# Seed 63 on the AMD Ryzen 7 PRO 8840HS - the batch's first run, free: the small end the best yet, 32 bits the best yet, the fast end lost - the JIT never assembled (Iteration 441)

`seed 63 10h widths noevolved then experiment compare-fronts.py --rounds 6 --widths 8,4 --frontier --cpus 2,8` at
863f35005 (Iteration 434): the after-seed-62 batch's first run, free of the tag-2 requirement. The remote checks started
17:08:11, the evolution 17:14:14; it evolved 6 h 37 min. 23,151 designs, generations 0-90: 18,624 alive, 4,524 screened
(20.6%), 3 that did not compile (below), none refused at the reach limit.

**Verdict: not small** - against seeds 59-62, both CPUs agreeing: fastest **-55.1%**, smallest **+10.80%**, memory +8 KB,
hypervolume **+15.1%**, best Q -20.7%; at 32 bits its fastest 53608880ce 0.293, **+13.1%** - the best yet. Its smallest,
14,348 bytes, is within rule 6's bound (14,535); its fastest, 0.263 (ec53b0ae91), is 2.4 times seed 62's 0.116.

**Why the fast end fell: the JIT never assembled.** The JIT needs the two-bit tag (jit_on, Iteration 80), and the
hand-made founder that carries the JIT (s6-cv8b with jit, jitx, jitloop, jitinl, Iteration 155) has tag2 off - in a
free run with no evolved founders its JIT never expresses. Seeds 59-62 required the tag, seed 58 had founders from
earlier runs; seed 63 had neither. The tag stayed at 3-6% of the designs, the JIT at 1-4%, and jitx, jitinl and jittail
never reached the front (the JIT on 10 of its 104, at best 0.269). Not a fault of the batch: seed 62's fastest JIT
designs measure the same on seed 62's code and on today's (0.163 and 0.153 against 0.153 and 0.155, here).

**Facts.** Its front: 104 designs - 94 plain CV8, 10 tag-2; tos on 82; every design with a 32-bit speed (speed against
32-bit speed: Spearman 0.942). No 32-bit failure of any kind (seeds 59-62: 1,780-2,148 a run). Three designs died at
compile: jit.c:598, `jit_ends` undeclared - a JIT gene combination (below, a fault to fix). heldout (sieve): Spearman
0.854, the fastest third 0.585; the pick's regret +7.5%. Fast-end optimism 1.051. The smallest still gaining at
generation 84 of 90; the best speed's last 2% at 49. Its chain's remote checks, of seed 62's front (results/
xcheck-seed63.md): the Tegra built all 7 designs asked for - the four that failed there before among them; the head to
head ranks two of seed 62's own front designs first across the machine classes.

---
# Evolved VM designs

18627 designs evaluated, 18624 alive, 4524 screened - not measured (Iteration 200). Speed is the geometric mean of median cpu time / hand-made s6, long workloads (Iteration 100) over kernel, fib, corpus, loop, bubble, ackermann,
relative to s6-cv8b; size is the self-hosting image, binary the stripped engine. sieve, parse is held out.
The front is over three objectives, equal (Iteration 75): speed, binary + image, peak memory - memory within 8 KB a tie (Iteration 77).


The Q-best design (binary + image x run time x memory - the knee; information): 04263875e4, speed 0.318, binary + image 22,145, memory 124 KB - Q 0.265 of s6-cv8b's.
